# Probe scope

Which routine readings an emulator probe can check (D-012; generated by `python -I tools/probe_scope.py --doc docs/PROBE_SCOPE.md`). A probe runs one routine on tools/sm83.py from a chosen register and RAM state and compares registers, flags and RAM with its note (config/probes/, tests/test_probes.py). A routine is in scope when it and everything it reaches touch only CPU registers, WRAM, HRAM, SRAM and the memory-bank registers. The others are listed with the reason: what they do shows on the display, in sound, on the serial link or the joypad (registers or video RAM), or depends on interrupt timing, or jumps through a computed address.

Limits (E-0386): a probe reads WRAM bank 1 after the routine returns, so a write a routine makes to WRAM bank 2 (the CGB attribute buffers) is not observed; those parts of a probed note stay C1 and its open questions say so. The classification goes by the symbols an instruction names, so a video-RAM symbol used as a plain number (TalkMsg0A3C_KingIfYouSee loads 40,000 as `vBGMap1 + $40`) puts a routine out of scope although it touches no hardware; such routines were probed where a worker noticed it.
